Rendering A (fable-5-1)
For if it is mixed with a drying thing, it renders the whole drying, and if with a moistening thing, it makes this too moistening; and often the mixture of both has been rendered even more moistening than the very thing that moistens primarily, as is the case with water. For water moistens primarily, but, because it does not remain for any length of time, having an activity of short duration, it benefits only weakly. Oil, therefore, becoming a bond for it, since it compels it to remain in contact for the longest possible time with those who need the help that comes from it, has been rendered the best of all remedies for bodies that are dried out and parched. In the same way, if you mix a drying drug with oil, such as chalcitis, or pellitory, or oak-gall, or soapwort, the mixture of both will be drying. Oil, then, since it serves all the things that are mixed with it, is reasonably, as it were, a kind of material of all of them. It necessarily diminishes their proper activity, but not indeed their benefit; rather, there are times when it even increases it. For it is certainly not the same thing, even if it will seem so to some, to increase the activity of something and to increase its benefit.

ἡ μὲν γὰρ ἐνέργεια κατὰ τὴν οἰκείαν ἑκάστου δύναμιν, ἡ δ’ ὠφέλεια ﻿ 534 καὶ κατὰ συμβεβηκὸς ἐνίοτε. λεχθήσεται μὲν οὖν ἐπιπλέον ἐν τοῖς ἑξῆς ὑπὲρ τῆς κατὰ συμβεβηκὸς ὠφελείας. εἰρήσεται δὲ καὶ νῦν εἰς τοσοῦτον, εἰς ὅσον δεῖ τοῖς παροῦσιν. ἐλαίῳ γὰρ ἀνακοπτόμενον ὕδωρ, ὡς τὸ καλούμενον ὑδρέλαιον ἐργάσασθαι, τοῖς κεκοπωμένοις ἀρήγει μᾶλλον ἢ εἰ μόνον ὑπῆρχεν, οὐ τῷ τὴν οἰκείαν ἐπιτείνεσθαι δύναμιν, ἀλλὰ τὸ περιμένειν ἐπιπολὺ τοῖς δεομένοις. Ὅτι δ’ εἰ μὴ μέσον εἴη τῇ κράσει τὸ ξυνδοῦν, τὰ πρώτως βοη θοῦντα τοῖς δεομένοις ἐναντιώσεται πολλάκις αὐτῶν ταῖς ἐνεργείαις, ἐξ αὐτῆς γε τῆς τῶν φαρμάκων φύσεως ἔνεστί σοι καθόλου διδαχθῆναι κᾀκ τῶν τῆς κατ’ εἶδος τῶν φαρμάκων ἱστορίας. ἀπὸ μὲν τῆς τοῦ καθόλου, διότι τὸ μιγνύμενον ἂν ἐπὶ τοὐναντίον ἀπάγῃ τὴν δύναμιν τοῦ κραθέντος αὐτῷ φαρμάκου, δύναταί ποτε καὶ νικῆσαι καὶ τελέως εἰς ἕτερον εἶδος μεταστῆσαι τὸ μικτὸν ἐξ ἀμφοῖν.
Rendering A (fable-5-1) · unsure: τὰ πρώτως βοηθοῦντα τοῖς δεομένοις ἐναντιώσεται πολλάκις αὐτῶν ταῖς ἐνεργείαις, τῶν τῆς κατ’ εἶδος τῶν φαρμάκων ἱστορίας, τὸ ξυνδοῦν
For the activity is in accordance with the proper power of each thing, whereas the benefit 534 is sometimes also by accident. Now, more will be said at greater length in what follows about benefit by accident; but it will be stated now too, to the extent that is required for the present matters. For water beaten up with oil, so as to produce what is called hydrelaion, helps those who are fatigued more than if it were present alone, not because its proper power is intensified, but because it remains for a long time on those who need it. And that, if the binding agent is not intermediate in its mixture, it will often oppose, in their activities, the things that primarily help those who need them — this you can be taught in general from the very nature of drugs, and also from the record of drugs according to species. From the general consideration, because the thing admixed, if it draws the power of the drug blended with it toward the opposite, can sometimes even prevail and completely transfer the mixture of both into another kind.

For the activity is in accordance with the proper power of each thing, whereas the benefit 534 sometimes arises also by accident. More will be said at greater length in what follows about benefit by accident; but it will be stated now as well, to the extent that the present matter requires. For water beaten up with oil, so as to produce what is called hydrelaeon, helps those suffering from fatigue more than if it were on its own, not because its proper power is intensified, but because it remains for a long time upon those who need it. That, if the binding agent were not intermediate in its mixture, it would often oppose the activities of the things that primarily help those in need, this you can learn both in general from the very nature of drugs and from the record of drugs according to their species. From the general consideration, because what is mixed in, if it draws the power of the drug blended with it towards the contrary, can at times even overcome it and change the mixture of both completely into another kind.

Greek (Kühn)
οὐδεμίαν γὰρ οὔτε ἰδέαν ἐπίσημον οὔτε δύναμιν ἐμφαίνεσθαι χρὴ τοῦ λόγον ὕλης ἔχοντος 535 ἐν τῇ μίξει. κατὰ δὲ τὴν ἀπὸ τῶν ἐν μέρει πεῖραν ᾧδ’ ἂν μάθοις ὡς οὐ χρὴ τὸ λόγον ὕλης ἔχον οὔτε θερμαίνειν οὔτε ψύχειν οὔτε ξηραίνειν οὔθ’ ὑγραίνειν, εἰ πρῶτον μὲν ἀναμνησθείης ὧν ὀλίγον ἔμπροσθεν ἐμνημόνευσα ἐπὶ πίττης ὑγρᾶς καὶ ῥητίνης καὶ σμύρνης, ἀσφάλτου τε καὶ κεδρίνου καὶ δαφνίνου καὶ τῶν ἄλλων ὅσα θερμαίνει. ταῦτα γὰρ ἅπαντα μεθ’ ὕδατος ἀνατριβόμενα παραπλησίως ἐλαίῳ θερμαίνει μᾶλλον ἢ ψύχει καὶ ξηραίνει μᾶλλον ἢ ὑγραίνει, διὸ καὶ τὰς κοπώδεις αἰσθήσεις οὐ πάνυ τι λύειν πέφυκεν. οὔτε γὰρ τῆς αὐτῆς ἐστιν ὕδατι δυνάμεως, ὡς συνεργάζεσθαί τι μᾶλλον ἢ ἀντιπράττειν, οὔτε τῆς μέσης τῶν ἄκρων, ἵν’ ὡς ὕλη τις ὑπηρετῇ τῷ πρώτως ἐνεργοῦντι. ξηραίνει γὰρ καὶ θερμαίνει μᾶλλον, οὐχ ὑγραίνει, καὶ ψύχει καθάπερ ὕδωρ. εἰ δέ τινι τῶν ἰσχυρῶς ξηραινόντων τε καὶ θερμαινόντων ἀναμίξαις ὕδωρ, οὐ μόνον οὐδὲν ὧν πέφυκε δρᾷν ὄψει γιγνόμενον, ἀλλὰ καὶ τελέως ἀμαυρούμενον ὡς εἰ μηδὲ τὴν ἀρχὴν ἐμέμικτο.
Rendering A (fable-5-1)
For that which has the role of matter in the mixture ought to display neither any notable form nor any power 535. And from the experience drawn from particulars you would learn as follows that what has the role of matter ought neither to heat nor to cool nor to dry nor to moisten: if, first, you were to recall what I mentioned a little earlier concerning liquid pitch and resin and myrrh, bitumen and cedar-oil and laurel-oil and all the other things that heat. For all of these, when rubbed up with water in the same manner as oil, heat rather than cool and dry rather than moisten, and for this reason they are not by nature very apt to relieve the sensations of fatigue. For they are neither of the same power as water, so as to cooperate with it in some way rather than to act against it, nor of the power midway between the extremes, so that they might serve, like a kind of matter, that which acts primarily. For they dry and heat rather; they do not moisten and cool as water does. But if you were to mix water with one of the things that strongly dry and heat, you will see not only none of the things it naturally does coming about, but even water itself completely obscured, as if it had not been mixed in at all in the first place.
Rendering B (opus-5-5) · unsure: ἰδέαν, λόγον ὕλης ἔχοντος, ὧν πέφυκε δρᾷν
For that which has the role (logos) of matter must display no distinctive quality (idea) or power 535 in the mixture. From experience of the particulars you would learn as follows that what has the role of matter must neither heat nor cool nor dry nor moisten, if you first recall what I mentioned a little earlier concerning liquid pitch, resin and myrrh, asphalt, cedar oil and laurel oil, and all the other things that heat. For all of these, when rubbed up with water in the same way as oil, heat rather than cool and dry rather than moisten; for this reason they are not at all naturally suited to relieve sensations of fatigue. For such a substance is neither of the same power as water, so as to cooperate with it somewhat rather than counteract it, nor of the power intermediate between the extremes, so as to serve as a kind of matter for what acts primarily. For it rather dries and heats, and does not moisten and cool as water does. But if you mix water into one of the things that dry and heat strongly, you will see not only that none of the things it naturally does takes place, but that it is completely obscured, as if it had not been mixed in at all.

χαλκῖτις οὖν καὶ σανδαράχη καὶ μίσυ 536 καὶ τἄλλ’ ὅσα θερμαίνει τε σφοδρῶς ἡμᾶς καὶ ξηραίνει, φυλάττει τὴν ἑαυτῶν ἔτι δύναμιν ἰσχυρὰν ὕδατι μιχθέντα. πανταχόθεν οὖν δῆλον ὡς μέσον εἶναι χρὴ τὴν κρᾶσιν ὅτί περ ἂν ἑκατέροις τοῖς ἄκροις ὑπηρετήσειν μέλλῃ, καθάπερ ὕλη τις, ὥσπερ καὶ τοὔλαιον ἐν τῇδε τῇ χώρᾳ τιθεὶς οὐκ ἂν ἁμάρτοις. ὁμολογήσει γὰρ ἅπαντά σοι τὰ κατὰ μέρος ἔργα καὶ μέμψῃ τοῦ μήκους τῶν λόγον οὐχ ἡμᾶς, ἀλλ’ ὅσοι τὴν ὀρθὴν ὁδὸν ὑπερβαίνοντες ἐπὶ μοχθηρᾶς τε καὶ μακρᾶς ἐκτρέπονται. διὸ καὶ τοῦτον ἠναγκάσθην ἐγὼ μηκῦναι τὸν λόγον, ἐπεὶ τό γ’ ἀληθὲς αὐτὸ ταχίστην ἔχει τὴν εὕρεσιν, ὡς ἂν ἐξ αὐτῶν τῶν ἐναργῶς φαινομένων ὁρμώμενον. οὐ μὴν οὐδ’ εἰς τοσοῦτον ἐμήκυνα τὸν λόγον εἰς ὅσον ἐγχωρεῖ τῷ καὶ πάντα τὰ κακῶς ὑπ’ αὐτῶν εἰρημένα γράψαι προῃρημένῳ καὶ τὰς αἰτίας τῶν σφαλμάτων ἐκδιδάξαι. καίτοι γε τὰς αἰτίας εἰπεῖν ἴσως ἀναγκαῖόν ἐστιν ὑπὲρ τοῦ φυλάττεσθαί τε τοὺς ἐξ αὐτῶν παραλογισμοὺς ἐν ἁπάσῃ τῇ κρίσει τῶν κατὰ μέρος φαρμάκων ἔχεσθαί τε τῆς ἀληθοῦς διδασκαλίας ἐπιστημονικώτερον.
Rendering A (fable-5-1) · unsure: τῶν λόγον
Chalcitis, then, and sandarach and misy 536 and all the others that heat and dry us vehemently still preserve their own power strong when mixed with water. From every side, then, it is clear that whatever is going to serve each of the two extremes, like a kind of matter, must be intermediate in its mixture; and so, in placing oil too in this position, you would not go wrong. For all the particular effects will agree with you, and you will blame for the length of the argument not us, but all those who, stepping beyond the straight road, turn aside onto a bad and long one. For this reason I too have been compelled to lengthen this discussion, since the truth itself admits of a very swift discovery, inasmuch as it sets out from the very things that evidently appear. Yet I have not lengthened the discussion to such an extent as is possible for one who has chosen to write down all the things badly said by them and to teach thoroughly the causes of their errors. And yet to state the causes is perhaps necessary, in order both to guard against the fallacies arising from them in the whole judgement of particular drugs and to hold to the true teaching in a more scientific manner.
Rendering B (opus-5-5) · unsure: τῶν λόγον (apparently corrupt for τῶν λόγων; rendered 'of the discussions'), μοχθηρᾶς τε καὶ μακρᾶς (noun understood as ὁδούς, 'roads')
Thus chalcitis, sandarach and misy 536 and all the other things that heat and dry us violently still preserve their own power strong when mixed with water. From every side, then, it is clear that whatever is going to serve both extremes, like a kind of matter, must be intermediate in its mixture (krasis); and in placing oil too in this position you would not err. For all the particular effects will agree with you, and for the length of the discussions you will blame not us, but those who overstep the right road and turn aside onto roads that are both bad and long. This is why I too was compelled to lengthen this account, since the truth itself is found most quickly, inasmuch as it starts out from the very things that appear evidently. Yet I have not lengthened the account as far as is possible for one who has chosen both to write out everything that has been wrongly said by them and to teach in full the causes of their errors. And yet it is perhaps necessary to state the causes, so that one may both guard against the paralogisms arising from them in the whole judgement of individual drugs and hold to the true teaching more scientifically.

537 For our convictions about matters are increased when we not only know the truth itself but also learn thoroughly the causes of all the fallacies. This, then, we shall work out in what follows. But now, having added a few things about rose oil to what was said at the beginning, I shall bring the discussion to a close at this point. It has been said, then, that it is not reasonable to attend to smells or colours or any other of the accidents in the examinations of powers, but rather to what is clearly apparent in both healthy and diseased bodies. And it has also been demonstrated that olive oil, when it is unsalted and entirely pure, belongs to the middle material. So that rose oil too—the precise and simple and truly rose oil—must be prepared from this oil and have nothing more drug-like in itself, as the perfumers are accustomed to prepare it, astringing the oil beforehand with aromatics before putting in the roses, so that it may become lasting and more fragrant.

537 For our convictions about things are strengthened when we not only know the truth itself but also learn thoroughly the causes of all the paralogisms. This, then, we shall do in what follows. For now, after adding a few words about rose oil to what was said at the beginning, I shall bring the discussion to an end here. It has also been demonstrated that oil, when it is unsalted and perfectly pure, is of the middle matter. So the rose oil too, the exact, simple and genuine rose oil, must be prepared from this oil and must contain nothing more drug-like in itself, as the perfumers are accustomed to prepare it: to make it lasting and more fragrant, they first astringe the oil with aromatics before putting in the roses.

κάλλιον δὲ ἴσως ἐστὶ καὶ τοῖς ὀνόμασιν διαστέλλειν αὐτὰ, 538 καθότι καὶ τοὺς παλαιοὺς οἶδα ποιοῦντας. ἔλαιον μὲν γὰρ ῥόδινον ὀνομάζουσιν τὸ χωρὶς τῶν ἀρωμάτων σκευασθέν. ὅτῳ δ’ ἐπεμβέβληταί τι καὶ τούτων, οὐκ ἔλαιον ἔτι τὸ τοιοῦτον, ἀλλὰ μύρον ἤδη προσαγορεύουσιν. ὥστε ἐπειδὴ τὸ μὲν ἔλαιον οἷον ὕλη τίς ἐστιν τῶν θερμαινόντων τε καὶ ψυχόντων φαρμάκων ἐκ τῆς μέσης κράσεως ὑπάρχον, ἐξ ἐλαίου δὲ καὶ ῥόδων χυλῶν σύγκειται τὸ ῥόδινον, ἔστι δήπου καὶ αὐτὸ τοιοῦτον τὴν κρᾶσιν οἷός περ καὶ ὁ τῶν ῥόδων χυλός. ἔστι δὲ δήπου ῥᾷστον οὐ ῥόδων χυλοῦ μόνον αὐτοῦ καθ’ ἑαυτὸν, ἀλλὰ καὶ συνθέντας ἔλαιον ῥόδινον ἐπί τε τῶν ὑγιαινόντων καὶ νοσούντων, ὡς ἤδη πολλάκις εἴρηται, τὴν βάσανον αὐτοῦ ποιήσασθαι τῆς δυνάμεως. εὑρήσεις οὖν ἐμψυχομένους μὲν ὑπ’ αὐτοῦ τοὺς ἔτι κεκαυμένους καὶ παραχρῆμά τινος ὠφελείας αἰσθανομένους, βλαπτομένους δ’ ἐναργῶς ἅπαντας τοὺς ἐψυγμένους, ὥστε σε σαφῶς ἑκατέρωθεν συνιέναι τῆς δυνάμεως αὐτοῦ ψυχούσης μᾶλλον ἢ θερμαινούσης.
Rendering A (fable-5-1)
But it is perhaps better to distinguish them by their names as well, 538 just as I know the ancients also did. For they call 'rose oil' that which has been prepared without the aromatics; but that to which something of these too has been added they no longer call oil, but by now 'unguent' (μύρον). And so, since oil is as it were a kind of matter for heating and cooling drugs, being itself of the middle mixture, and rose oil is composed of oil and the juices of roses, it too is surely of such a mixture as the juice of roses is. And it is surely very easy to make the test of its power—not only of the juice of roses alone by itself, but also, having compounded rose oil, on both healthy and diseased bodies, as has already often been said. You will find, then, that those who are still burning are cooled by it and immediately perceive some benefit, while all those who have been chilled are manifestly harmed, so that from both sides you clearly understand that its power is cooling rather than heating.
Rendering B (opus-5-5) · unsure: ἔτι κεκαυμένους, μύρον, ἐπεμβέβληται
But it is perhaps better to distinguish them by their names as well, 538 just as I know the ancients also did. For they name as rose oil that which has been prepared without the aromatics; but that into which some of these too has been added they no longer call oil but now call an unguent. So, since oil is, as it were, a kind of matter for heating and cooling drugs, being of the middle mixture, and rose oil is compounded of oil and the juices of roses, it is surely itself of the same mixture as the juice of the roses is. And it is surely very easy to make the test of its power, not only of the juice of roses itself on its own, but also, having compounded rose oil, on both healthy and sick bodies, as has already been said many times. You will find, then, that those who are still burnt are cooled by it and at once perceive some benefit, while all those who have been chilled are manifestly harmed, so that from both sides you clearly understand that its power cools rather than heats.

Greek (Kühn)
οὐ μὴν ἰσχυρά γε ἡ ἀπὸ ῥοδίνου φαίνεταί σοι ψύξις, ἀλλ’ ἐγγυτάτω πως εἶναι 539 δόξει τῆς μέσης κράσεως. ὀνομάζω δὲ τὰ τοιαῦτα φάρμακα σαφοῦς ἕνεκα διδασκαλίας ἐκ τῆς πρώτης φύσεώς τε καὶ τάξεως τῶν ψυχόντων, ὥσπερ αὖ καὶ ὅσα βραχὺ τῆς μέσης κράσεως ἐπὶ τὸ θερμότερον ἀποκεχώρηκεν, ἐκ τῆς πρώτης ἀποστάσεώς τε καὶ τάξεως τῶν θερμαινόντων. ταυτὶ μὲν ἐν τῷ παρόντι περὶ ῥοδίνου χρὴ γινώσκειν. ἀναγκαῖον γὰρ ἔσται μοι καὶ κατὰ τὸν ἑξῆς λόγον ἔτι μνημονεῦσαί τε τοῦ φαρμάκου καὶ προσθεῖναι τὰ λείποντα ὧν εἴπομεν.
Rendering A (fable-5-1) · unsure: φύσεώς
Nevertheless, the cooling produced by rose oil does not appear to you strong, but it will seem to be, in a way, very close 539 to the middle mixture. For the sake of clear teaching I name such drugs as belonging to the first nature and order of cooling agents, just as, again, I name those which have departed a little from the middle mixture toward the hotter as belonging to the first remove and order of heating agents. These, then, are the things one must know at present about rose oil. For it will be necessary for me, in the discourse that follows as well, to make mention of the drug once more and to add what is lacking in what we have said.
Rendering B (opus-5-5) · unsure: φύσεώς (in ἐκ τῆς πρώτης φύσεώς τε καὶ τάξεως: the parallel phrase ἐκ τῆς πρώτης ἀποστάσεώς τε καὶ τάξεως suggests ἀποστάσεως, 'remove/degree', may be the intended reading)
Yet the cooling produced by rose oil will not appear strong to you; rather, it will seem to be in some way very close 539 to the middle mixture. For the sake of clear teaching I name such drugs as being of the first nature and order of the cooling drugs, just as, conversely, I name those that have departed a little from the middle mixture towards the hotter as being of the first remove and order of the heating drugs. This, then, is what one must know about rose oil for the present. For it will be necessary for me in the following discourse as well to mention this drug again and to add what is lacking from what we have said.
